Writing the real table in OnValidate or OnOpenPage commits a partial agent when the dialog errors or is cancelled. ([waldo1001/waldo.BCObservatory](https://github.com/waldo1001/waldo.BCObservatory/blob/HEAD/content/guidelines/community/knowledge/agents/agent-setup-source-table-is-temporary.md) · ★15)

- **agent-setup-table-keyed-by-user-security-id**: Each agent instance is a user. Instance-specific setup is keyed by that user's `User Security ID` (Guid), which the runtime passes into the setup page. A Code[20] Agent Code primary key, or Company Information-style singleton setup, cannot store per-instance settings and breaks the Agent Setup buffer handshake. ([microsoft/BCQuality](https://github.com/microsoft/BCQuality/blob/HEAD/community/knowledge/agents/agent-setup-table-keyed-by-user-security-id.md) · ★247 · 6 copies)

- **agent-team-state-lives-under-claude-teams-directory**: Claude Code's agent-teams feature writes its full state to disk under a per-team folder: a config file, a tasks folder, and an inboxes folder holding every message passed between teammates. - **agent-teams**: A team is one **lead** and its **members**. The lead is a Claude session that talks to you, picks tickets and starts one member per ticket, each in its own worktree. - **agent_14**: **Key Findings** (bulleted, quantified) - A flat 79 USD per clinic per month (GIVEN) is a workable anchor, but it is mis-shaped: - It charges a solo-dentist clinic the same as a 6-chair clinic, which leaves money on the table from larger clinics. - It bundles an uncapped variable cost (reminders) into a fixed fee. - It ignores the local-currency, VAT-inclusive way Gulf clinics compare prices. Conf ([Waleed178-del/spm-ultra](https://github.com/Waleed178-del/spm-ultra/blob/HEAD/examples/dental-saas-gcc/agents/agent_14.md))
- **agent_15**: **Key Findings** (bulleted, quantified) - Method: - 5-year annual free cash flows, plus a terminal value at a multiple of Year-5 ARR. - Discount rate **40%** (ASSUMED seed-stage venture hurdle rate). - Contribution margin 63% at an ARPA of 79 USD (GIVEN price). - CapEx of 33,000 USD in Year 1. - Fixed costs ~25,000 USD/month in Year 1. - All inputs are ASSUMED unless noted. - **Plan case (300 clin ([Waleed178-del/spm-ultra](https://github.com/Waleed178-del/spm-ultra/blob/HEAD/examples/dental-saas-gcc/agents/agent_15.md))
- **agent_16**: Premise (Klein premortem): it is November 2027. The company has failed: well short of 300 paying clinics, close to out of cash, and the founder is looking for a bridge round that nobody will lead. The causes below are worked back from that outcome and ranked by how much each contributed. ([Waleed178-del/spm-ultra](https://github.com/Waleed178-del/spm-ultra/blob/HEAD/examples/dental-saas-gcc/agents/agent_16.md))
- **agent_17**: Method: ISO 31000 risk identification, analysis and evaluation. Probability (P) is the 12-month likelihood. Impact (I) is on a 1-5 scale, where 5 = the company cannot reach its goal or survive. Score = P x I, with P as a fraction. Tiers: **Catastrophic** = score &gt;=2.5 or I=5 with P&gt;=40%; **Medium** = 1.0-2.5; **Low** = &lt;1.0. ([Waleed178-del/spm-ultra](https://github.com/Waleed178-del/spm-ultra/blob/HEAD/examples/dental-saas-gcc/agents/agent_17.md))
- **agent_18**: Scope: data privacy, health-data rules, telecom/messaging and tax exposure for a scheduling SaaS that processes patient names, phone numbers and appointment reasons for dental clinics in the UAE and KSA. WEB MODE OFF: legal references come from analyst knowledge and must be verified by local counsel. All cost figures are ASSUMED. ([Waleed178-del/spm-ultra](https://github.com/Waleed178-del/spm-ultra/blob/HEAD/examples/dental-saas-gcc/agents/agent_18.md))
- **agent_19**: Method: SRE-style dependency audit (Beyer et al.). For each single dependency: the share of the business it carries, the redundancy that exists, the time to replace it, and the blast radius if it fails. ([Waleed178-del/spm-ultra](https://github.com/Waleed178-del/spm-ultra/blob/HEAD/examples/dental-saas-gcc/agents/agent_19.md))
- **agent_20**: Method: ISO 22301 business impact analysis (MTPD/RTO/RPO), with NIST SP 800-34 contingency tiers covering technical, people and financial continuity. Includes emergency pivot criteria. ([Waleed178-del/spm-ultra](https://github.com/Waleed178-del/spm-ultra/blob/HEAD/examples/dental-saas-gcc/agents/agent_20.md))
- **AGENT_20_CHECKLIST**: **Version:** 1.0.0 **Role:** Quality Gatekeeper **Responsibility:** Prevent premature "done" reporting ([seanchatmangpt/erlmcp](https://github.com/seanchatmangpt/erlmcp/blob/HEAD/docs/agents/AGENT_20_CHECKLIST.md))
- **agent_21**: **Key Findings** - The target needs a sellable product early. 300 paying clinics (GIVEN) in 12 months (GIVEN) means about 33 net new clinics a month if selling starts in month 3 (ASSUMED start). If the MVP slips to month 5, that rises to about 43 a month. Every month of build delay costs about 3-4 extra clinics a month on the rest of the sales curve. **High confidence.** - Build capacity is 2 engi ([Waleed178-del/spm-ultra](https://github.com/Waleed178-del/spm-ultra/blob/HEAD/examples/dental-saas-gcc/agents/agent_21.md))
- **agent_22**: **Key Findings** - **C4 context level.** The system has four outside dependencies: (1) the WhatsApp Business Platform through Meta or a Business Solution Provider (BSP), (2) an SMS gateway as fallback, (3) a subscription payment gateway, and (4) a cloud host. No PMS or EHR integration is in the MVP. Each dependency is a separate failure domain. WhatsApp is the one the value proposition rests on. * ([Waleed178-del/spm-ultra](https://github.com/Waleed178-del/spm-ultra/blob/HEAD/examples/dental-saas-gcc/agents/agent_22.md))
- **agent_23**: **Key Findings** - **There are three journeys, and each has its own friction budget.** (1) Clinic onboarding: owner or receptionist, once per clinic. (2) Daily reception use: the receptionist, 20-30 bookings a day (ASSUMED). (3) The patient's reminder reply and self-booking: on mobile, often older patients, mostly in Arabic. Journey 3 decides whether no-shows actually fall. **High confidence.** - ([Waleed178-del/spm-ultra](https://github.com/Waleed178-del/spm-ultra/blob/HEAD/examples/dental-saas-gcc/agents/agent_23.md))
- **agent_24**: **Key Findings** - **Load baseline (ASSUMED: 25 appointments per clinic per day, 24 working days a month, 2 reminders per appointment).** ([Waleed178-del/spm-ultra](https://github.com/Waleed178-del/spm-ultra/blob/HEAD/examples/dental-saas-gcc/agents/agent_24.md))
- **agent_25**: **Key Findings** - **Kraljic classification of the vendor stack:** - *Strategic* (high impact, few suppliers): the **WhatsApp Business Platform**. Meta is the only source. BSPs only resell access and add tooling. Lock-in sits with the channel itself, not the BSP. **High confidence.** - *Bottleneck* (lower spend, hard to substitute): **in-country cloud hosting** in KSA, and a **KSA payment gateway* ([Waleed178-del/spm-ultra](https://github.com/Waleed178-del/spm-ultra/blob/HEAD/examples/dental-saas-gcc/agents/agent_25.md))
- **agent_26**: **Key Findings** (bulleted, quantified) - Addressable base (ASSUMED): ~2,000-3,000 independent dental clinics in the UAE and ~4,000-6,000 in Saudi Arabia, so ~6,000-9,000 total. Basis: dental clinic counts scaled from population (UAE ~10M, KSA ~35M), with roughly 60-70% assumed non-chain. Would be confirmed by DHA / DOH Abu Dhabi / MOHAP facility registries and the Saudi MOH / SCFHS licensed-facil ([Waleed178-del/spm-ultra](https://github.com/Waleed178-del/spm-ultra/blob/HEAD/examples/dental-saas-gcc/agents/agent_26.md))
- **agent_27**: **Key Findings** (bulleted, quantified) - Capacity gap (High confidence). Reaching 300 paying clinics (GIVEN) in 12 months with an ASSUMED ~2.5% monthly logo churn needs ~330-350 gross wins. If the product is sellable from month 3 (ASSUMED, given 2 engineers starting at MVP stage), there are 9 selling months, so ~37-39 wins per month. A single founder in field sales can close an ASSUMED 8-15 clini ([Waleed178-del/spm-ultra](https://github.com/Waleed178-del/spm-ultra/blob/HEAD/examples/dental-saas-gcc/agents/agent_27.md))
- **agent_28**: **Key Findings** (bulleted, quantified) - "Appointment scheduling software" is a crowded frame (ASSUMED competitive alternatives): - global booking widgets (Calendly-style, generic); - regional clinic marketplaces and booking apps that list clinics for patients; - full PMS suites with a scheduling module; - the status quo: a paper diary plus a receptionist on WhatsApp. At 79 USD per month (GIVEN), ([Waleed178-del/spm-ultra](https://github.com/Waleed178-del/spm-ultra/blob/HEAD/examples/dental-saas-gcc/agents/agent_28.md))
- **agent_29**: **Key Findings** (bulleted, quantified) - ARPA: 79 USD per month (GIVEN). Gross margin is ASSUMED at 75-82%, because WhatsApp and SMS message fees (~5-10 USD per clinic per month, ASSUMED), hosting and support time eat into the margin. Gross-margin contribution is therefore ~60-65 USD per clinic per month. - Churn: ASSUMED 2-3.5% per month logo churn, typical for SMB vertical SaaS sold monthly. Ex ([Waleed178-del/spm-ultra](https://github.com/Waleed178-del/spm-ultra/blob/HEAD/examples/dental-saas-gcc/agents/agent_29.md))
- **agent_30**: **Key Findings** (bulleted, quantified) - Churn sensitivity (ASSUMED). At 300 clinics (GIVEN), each 1 point of monthly churn loses ~3 clinics per month, which is about 2,850 USD of ARR per month. Moving churn from 4% to 2% per month turns a gross need of ~370 wins into ~335 to land 300 net. (Medium confidence) - Churn drivers in small clinics (ASSUMED, from patterns in SMB vertical SaaS): - the re ([Waleed178-del/spm-ultra](https://github.com/Waleed178-del/spm-ultra/blob/HEAD/examples/dental-saas-gcc/agents/agent_30.md))

- **agenta**: Architecture judge grounded in an empirical corpus of 99 real hackathon winner repos plus the Razorpay rubric. Scores form against what judges actually read, substance against the bar Razorpay actually applies, and refuses to reward documentation polish that the data shows does not correlate with winning. ([NithishaVenkatesh/upi-agent-conformance](https://github.com/NithishaVenkatesh/upi-agent-conformance/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/agenta.md))
- **agentarch**: Forensic repository analyst. Reconstructs a project's real architecture from code (never from README claims), then extracts transferable engineering patterns scored against the Razorpay AI Buildathon bar. Adversarial by mandate — winning does not mean well-engineered. ([NithishaVenkatesh/upi-agent-conformance](https://github.com/NithishaVenkatesh/upi-agent-conformance/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/agentarch.md))
- **agentation-fix-loop**: Load when the user is running an Agentation two-session workflow and you are the Session 2 (fix) side. Watches MCP annotations from the critique session, reads each annotation's selector + file path + critique, applies the fix in code, commits, and loops to the next. Use only when the agentation MCP server is connected. ([AgentsORG/design-engineering](https://github.com/AgentsORG/design-engineering/blob/HEAD/plugin/harness/opencode/agents/agentation-fix-loop.md) · ★12 · 4 copies)
- **agentation-reviewer**: Reviews changes against agentation-html's hard-won invariants (the CLAUDE.md lessons) — exact @types/bun pin, hoisted linker, watchman-from-PATH, legacy marker retention, transform purity/idempotency, hermetic tests, Watchman realpath embedding. Use before committing changes to bin/, src/, package.json, bunfig.toml, or the CI/test setup. ([itsbrex/agentation-html](https://github.com/itsbrex/agentation-html/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/agentation-reviewer.md))
- **agentbox-builder**: Implement one scoped slice inside an isolated worktree against written acceptance criteria. Reports observed command output and baselines; stops at first failure. ([duythien0912/agentbox](https://github.com/duythien0912/agentbox/blob/HEAD/plugins/agentbox/agents/agentbox-builder.md))
- **agentbox-code-reviewer**: Review changed code on a branch and resolve Critical and High findings in a single pass. ([duythien0912/agentbox](https://github.com/duythien0912/agentbox/blob/HEAD/plugins/agentbox/agents/agentbox-code-reviewer.md))
- **agentbox-docs-writer**: Document completed changes into docs/changes/ with structured frontmatter, solution architecture, and tradeoff summaries. Triggered by conductor DocsGate (--agent-docs) or manual release documentation. ([duythien0912/agentbox](https://github.com/duythien0912/agentbox/blob/HEAD/plugins/agentbox/agents/agentbox-docs-writer.md))
- **agentbox-herdr-orchestrator**: Orchestrate multi-agent sessions across external panes. ([duythien0912/agentbox](https://github.com/duythien0912/agentbox/blob/HEAD/plugins/agentbox/agents/agentbox-herdr-orchestrator.md))
- **agentbox-mobile-verifier**: Verify mobile applications across React Native, Flutter, iOS, and Android simulators. Writes Maestro or Appium flows, or captures simctl and adb evidence. Triggered by conductor FrontendGate (--agent-mobile) or standalone mobile verification. ([duythien0912/agentbox](https://github.com/duythien0912/agentbox/blob/HEAD/plugins/agentbox/agents/agentbox-mobile-verifier.md))
- **agentbox-planner**: Decompose goals into grounded code slices and checkable acceptance criteria with baselines. Use for task planning or as the scoping lane of an orchestrated run. - **agentbox-test-writer**: Author failing acceptance tests prior to implementation. Translates acceptance criteria into runnable E2E or unit tests that fail for the targeted behavior. - **agentbox-tryve-enhancer**: Harden test coverage against implementation diffs. Generates tests for unhandled error paths, boundaries, auth, concurrency, and resource limits. - **agentbox-verifier**: Verify results independently at a named commit SHA with paired failure and pass proofs. Quantifies pass/fail without modifying code. ([duythien0912/agentbox](https://github.com/duythien0912/agentbox/blob/HEAD/plugins/agentbox/agents/agentbox-verifier.md))
